# cowork-sync Plan 5 (Claude Code integration) — Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Build 06-claude and the parts of 09-security it needs on the Plan 2 engine and the Plan 3 holds: the plugin package (`.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, `.mcp.json`, `hooks/hooks.json`, `commands/cowork.md`, the `dist/hook.js`, `dist/core.js` and `dist/mcp.js` bundles and their build lints); the fail-closed hook loader and core (frames, deadlines, outputs, non-live and paused fast paths, `pause`/`resume` markers, UserPromptExpansion evidence lines, worktree refusal); local IPC (key and ACL, endpoint, mutual HMAC handshake, schema, single-instance bind, squatter check); the edit-class gate G1–G4 with K1 deny-time records written first, SUSPECT refresh and the advisory shell gate; one lease table (INFLIGHT, READ, TXN) feeding ENG-31's defer set, the TXN skip and the writer's P1 re-check; hook reads (`diskSnapshotId`) and hook attribution windows; PostToolUse confirmation, K2 and the `wake-scope` hook point; edit checkpoints (trigger, candidate identity, per-CS queue, skips 1–6 and their `last_check` records); seats (identity, pairing, numbers, wiring and flags, activity, seat end, DEGRADED, NO_HOOKS with FREEZE `NOHOOKS`); the single turn-origin rule with doorbell rings and the change-set boundary signals; carriers with caps, pull delivery, state transitions to every seat, per-seat budgets with `PULL_MODE`, and the Stop block; the MCP server with its eight tools and token custody; daemon lifetime (spawn, seat and human leases, newest-version-wins, plugin/key/marker checks); the human-only acting-verb gate; and FakeHost for SIM. Plus roadmap row 5's rev 2.5 shares — F04 (human-activity marks with the CCI-37 nonce rule, return detection with this PC's `max(Δwall, Δmono)`, `meta.since` writes, `since_query`/`since_ran`, the recap on the loader `system` carrier, CCI-46 metering and the `PULL_MODE` shrink), F14 (the IPC `flow` selector, seat numbers, the `PULL_MODE`/`MID_TURN` read sets, the `fence`/`read_lease`/`txn` defer reasons), F10 (`cowork_show(cs_id)` undo view and its description clause), the F05 prerequisites — and owner decision D1 (widened). **Exit criterion (roadmap row 5, R5-15):** SIM with fake seats — O-LEASE, O-SEAT, O-TURN-ORIGIN and O-HUMANONLY pass (and O-EDITCHECK, which Plan 3 deferred here) on 1,000 `seats` seeds per OS; hook p95 ≤ 100 ms on Windows; T-F04-return, -restart and -since-first pass.

**Architecture:** One daemon process per clone hosts Plan 2's `Engine` and Plan 5's `ClaudeDaemon`; every hook is a short-lived `node dist/hook.js <Event>` process (the ≤ 150-line loader, which loads `dist/core.js` at run time with `await import('./core.js')`, A2-1); every Claude Code process runs one `node dist/mcp.js` stdio server; humans reach the daemon through `node dist/cli.js`. All four talk over one authenticated local endpoint. The source directories follow the one import order of every plan (X-110): `src/ports` → `src/text` → `src/i18n` → `src/git` → `src/sec` → `src/data` → `src/regions` → `src/board` → `src/since` → `src/holds` → `src/claims` → `src/engine` → `src/claude` → `src/coord` → `src/lifecycle` → `src/cli` → `src/daemon`, `src/entry` (`src/product` pure; `src/viz` under L-VIZ-IMPORT); an earlier-plan module never imports a later-plan module — a later plan implements a Plan 5 port type in its own file and the wiring lives in `src/daemon/run.ts` (X-111). Plan 5 adds `src/claude/**` after `src/engine` in three tiers: `src/claude/wire` and `src/claude/text` (pure contracts — frames, schema, constants, codes, hook-input parsing, shell tokenizer, `user.json` prefs, the Plan 5-only text helpers; the canonical root lives in `src/data/canonRoot.ts`, X-63, the data-directory rule in `src/data/pluginDataDir.ts`, X-61, and the Claude-facing renderer in `src/i18n/claude.ts`, X-54), `src/claude/client` (everything that runs inside a hook, MCP or CLI process; it never imports `src/engine`, `src/holds`, `src/board`, `src/data`, `src/git`, `src/regions` or `src/claude/daemon` — lint L-HOOK-THIN — so `core.js` stays thin enough for the 1.5 s deadline and the ≤ 100 ms p95), and `src/claude/daemon` (seats, leases, gate, turns, carriers, MCP handlers, the IPC server; it imports everything below it). The engine learns Plan 5's facts only through ports it already has or gains additively — `LeasePort` (Plan 2), `AttributionPort` (Plans 2/3), the new `ClaudeEnginePort` (defer set, TXN skip, P1 re-check, fenced paths) — and through `Engine.signal` (Plan 3). Later plans plug in through ports Plan 5 declares with a Plan 5 binding each (`RegionsPort` → Plan 4, with Plan 4's member names, X-20; `WakeScopePort`, `WakePolicy`, `CoordToolsPort`, `CoordHooksPort`, `DigestPort`, `SlotExtraPort`, `DueRequestsPort` → Plan 6 through its one `bindClaude(coordinator, ctx)` spread, X-38…X-42; `FlowPort`, `WrapPort`, `NoteScanPort`, the `Notifier` OS adapter, the other acting verbs and Plan 7's own IPC kinds → Plan 7, X-51, X-56, X-57, X-67). Plan 5 owns the Claude mechanics (carriers, caps, slots, metering, delivery, turn origin, nonce, ringing); Plan 6 owns the content and decisions (X-38). Plan 5 extends Plan 2's and Plan 3's files in place where a contract needs an additive member (R5-04); it never replaces a Plan 2/3 file, store or schema.

**Tech Stack:** unchanged from Plans 2–3 — Node ≥ 20 (`engines.node` `>=20.6` for contributors), TypeScript 5.x, esbuild (single-file ESM bundles, Plan 1 `build.mjs` `format: 'esm'` under `"type": "module"`; A2-1, SE5-16), `node:test` via `tsx`, `fast-check`, git ≥ 2.43, GitHub Actions on windows-latest, macos-latest and ubuntu-latest. No new dependency: IPC uses `node:net` (named pipes on Windows, Unix sockets elsewhere), the handshake and evidence tags `node:crypto` (`createHmac`, `timingSafeEqual`), event-loop measurement `node:perf_hooks` `monitorEventLoopDelay`; the MCP server speaks JSON-RPC 2.0 over stdio by hand (no MCP SDK; zero runtime dependencies, U11). `dist/hook.js` is built without bundling `core.js` (it stays a run-time `await import('./core.js')`, the ESM form of 06 §3.4.1 step 8's `require`; A2-1).

**Owner decisions (2026-10-08, final; spec rev 2.5 glossary §29.1/§29.8, 09 §3.11 and 10 §3.5.1 carry their final text, so Plan 5 edits no D-text, X-03):** **D1 (final)** — a failed edit check's last result is shown in full whenever `/cowork status --flow` or `/cowork wrap` runs, in a terminal or as a slash command (whose output that seat's Claude reads, as intended); the daemon does not test who asked, so a Claude Bash call of `cli.js status --flow` gets it too (a pull, not a push); it is never pushed to a carrier, a mirror, an OS notification or teammates. Plan 5 implements it with no gate (R5-14 as ruled by X-01). **D2** — a human-confirmed F10 revert is an intended shrink under HLD-50 for the blob it wrote only (Plan 3's kernel; Plan 5 changes nothing that composes with it, R5-28). **D3** — F32 is Plan 8a Task 19 (no Plan 5 effect). **D4 (reversed)** — the first public release happens only after Plan 8b; Plan 7 prepares and does not publish, the release is the last task of Plan 8b; no Plan 5 task publishes anything (R5-54; SE5-12 dropped, X-03). **D5** — F04-a (owner: the Plan 2 controller, F-P2-1, X-76) starts right after Plan 2; Plan 5 consumes its outputs (`src/since/report.ts`, `SinceMarks` and the `meta.since` parse, X-75); F04-a must have merged before Task 16 starts, because `sinceReport` content is an exit prerequisite (T-F04-return, -restart, R5-61); Plan 5 never declares `SinceMarks` or the `meta.since` parse in Plan 2 files and never writes a stand-in `src/since/report.ts` (R5-04, R5-61, X5-03, X5-38).

**Plan form (as Plans 2 and 3).** Ruling: this plan is design-complete, not code-complete — every task names its files, the exact interfaces it consumes and produces (§Interfaces), the spec rule ids it implements and every test by title with what it asserts; implementers write the code test-first from that contract — 18 tasks of this size written out as code would double the plan's volume — cost if wrong: an implementer misreads an interface, caught by the per-task review and by the next task's compile. Executed with superpowers:subagent-driven-development (a fresh implementer and a reviewer per task).

## Global Constraints

The constraints below are Plan 2's and Plan 3's, carried verbatim (they still bind), then the lines Plan 5 replaces, then Plan 5's own.

**Carried from Plan 1 (still binding):**
- Node floor **20** for everything under `src/` and `dist/` (no APIs newer than Node 20); dev tooling Node ≥ 20.6.
- Git floor **2.43.0** (glossary §18); `meetsFloor` rejects anything below it; `daemon.js run` refuses below it with `START_TOOL_VERSION`.
- **No runtime dependencies**; nothing under `src/` imports a package that is not a Node built-in.
- `dist/` stays the glossary §6 list; Plan 2 turns `dist/daemon.js` from a stub into the foreground daemon, the other five entry points stay stubs.
- Every git process is started by `createGitRunner(...).run(...)` (lint L-GIT-SPAWN); engine code calls git only through `GitRepo.run` (Task 2), which calls the runner.
- No `Date.now()`, `new Date()`, `performance.now()` outside `src/ports/clock.ts`; no `Math.random`, `crypto.randomBytes`, `randomUUID` outside `src/ports/random.ts` (L-CLOCK, L-RANDOM). `process.hrtime` is read only in `src/ports/clock.ts` (`machineMonoMs`).
- No `child_process` outside `src/git/runner.ts` in `src/` (L-PORTS). Plan 2 adds: no `fs`/`node:fs`/`fs/promises`/`node:fs/promises` import in `src/` outside `src/ports/fs.ts` (L-FS-PORT, Task 2).
- Every read and write of user and cowork files goes through the `Fs` port on `Buffer`s.
- Forced git environment (glossary §18): `LC_ALL=C`, `LANGUAGE=`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, `GCM_INTERACTIVE=never`, `SSH_ASKPASS_REQUIRE=never`, `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, stdin ignored unless a Buffer is piped.
- Watchdogs (glossary §18): fetch 45 s, push 90 s, read/hash commands 120 s; `update-ref`, `update-index`, `commit-tree`, `read-tree` are never killed.
- `enc(b)`: percent-encoding outside `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, ≤ 150 bytes, else refuse.
- Push rejections are classified by outcome after a fresh fetch (glossary §26.2 item 2; `classifyPush`); message text never decides.
- Files written by tools and fixtures: LF, UTF-8 without BOM, except fixtures that test BOM/CRLF.
- Commit messages end with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`.

**Git use (Plan 2):**
- **`--no-replace-objects` on every git call** (Plan 1 ledger carry): `buildGitInvocation` puts `--no-replace-objects` as `argv[0]` for all four classes, before `--git-dir`/`--work-tree` and the `-c` list, so `merge-tree`, `cat-file`, `read-tree`, `rev-list` and `write-tree` never see `refs/replace/*` (`GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS` stays never-listed). Plan 1's argv snapshots in `test/unit/flags.test.ts` change accordingly (Task 2).
- `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `--git-dir` and `--work-tree` are absolute; a relative value throws before spawning (ENG-08, 01-engine test 2).
- Every engine git call runs with cwd = the main-worktree root (filters name their scripts repo-relative, glossary §25 F12); a command that may run a filter never gets `--work-tree=<tmp/stage>` (ENG-08).
- **No git process reads a user's working file** (glossary §26.1 W1, ENG-27): content is read only by the staged read (01-engine §2) into `<gitdir>/cowork/tmp/stage/<p>`; listing commands (`ls-files -o --exclude-standard`, `check-attr`) may name the real work tree.
- Blobs come only from route (a) (private-index `add` of stage copies, unfiltered paths only), route (b) (`hash-object [-w] --path=<p>` of a stage copy or stdin, cwd = repo root) and route (c) (`hash-object [-w] --stdin --no-filters` for canon and concat output and for metadata blobs) (ENG-08, DAT-01).
- **Detection ticks write no object** (glossary §26.1 W6, ENG-04): objects are written only at ready-pair captures, publishes, applies and hold records. `update-index --refresh` never carries `-q`, runs only on a private or scratch index whose work tree is `tmp/stage`, and its result is read only from the `diff-files -z` that follows it on the same index (ENG-24, ENG-58).
- Code trees (A, `S_raw`, `S_disk`, `M_disk`, ready-pair `R`/`A_cap`, P, OUT, C) are produced only by ENG-10's four operations; metadata trees by `mktree` (ruling R-15).
- Never in daemon code: `git merge-base`, `-X …`, `--union`, `read-tree -u`/`-m`, `checkout`, `checkout-index`, `switch`, `restore`, `reset --hard`, `stash`, `apply`, `add -A`/`add .`, `push --atomic`, `--force`, a `+` push refspec, `git gc` (L-GIT-FORBIDDEN, L-MERGE-BASE, L-MERGE-OPTS, L-GIT-ATOMIC, L-REFS). The only non-fast-forward push is the human-typed recover, and it uses `--force-with-lease` (INV-FF).
- Every push refspec is built by `pushSpec()` (ENG-09, L-PUSH-ARGV); the only remote deletion is `deleteProbeRef()` (INV-OID); every push attempt goes through `governedPush()` and is counted (PRO-41).
- Every local ref update uses `--create-reflog` under `core.logAllRefUpdates=always` (refupdate class; fetch passes `-c core.logAllRefUpdates=always`) (ENG-02). The engine never writes `FETCH_HEAD`, `ORIG_HEAD`, `refs/remotes/*`, `refs/heads/*` or `.git/index` (INV-ENG-USER).
- Outcomes come from exit codes and `-z`/`--porcelain` output. The only reader of git's text is `readHttpThrottle` (Plan 2 Task 12), and its result can only start the throttle fallback (ENG-46, ENG-58, L-CLASSIFY-TEXT).
- Every git call carries `-c filter.<d>.required=true` for every discovered driver and `filter.lfs` (glossary §18, DAT-15), `-c attr.tree=<EPOCH_BASE>` on attribute-reading commands (DAT-13), `-c core.fsmonitor=false` and, on the private index, `-c core.untrackedCache=false` (ruling R-38).
- Every git call also carries `-c core.safecrlf=false` (controller ruling P2-SAFECRLF): a user's `core.safecrlf=true` makes `hash-object -w`/`add` die (rc 128) on CRLF↔LF conversion, which would read as a filter failure.
- Every git call also forces `-c i18n.commitEncoding=UTF-8` and `-c i18n.logOutputEncoding=UTF-8` (controller ruling P2-UTF8: a user's commitEncoding would put an `encoding` header and non-UTF-8 bytes into shared commits) and the environment `GIT_GRAFT_FILE=<hooksPath>/.no-grafts` (a path that never exists; `GIT_GRAFT_FILE` joins the never-list so it cannot be passed through) — controller ruling P2-GRAFTS: `.git/info/grafts` is still honoured under `--no-replace-objects` and would give one peer different first-parent chains.
- Every git call also forces `-c core.splitIndex=false` (ruling P2-SPLITINDEX: a user's split index would make scratch/private index calls write shared index files).

**Time:**
- Loop period **2.5 s** active (work pending or a capture due), **10 s** idle; one iteration at a time; a fetch starts ≥ 2.5 s after the previous fetch started, or right after it ends if it took longer; a fresh-fetch request during a fetch waits for that fetch (ENG-13, glossary §25 F10).
- Every timer is a Δ from a local `Anchor` (`anchorNow`, `elapsedMono`, `ownStaleness`, `suspendGapMs`); no wall-clock value from another machine is ever compared (INV-CLOCK).
- **Monotonic time across sleep is not trusted alone** (Plan 1 ledger carry): every iteration computes, against the previous iteration's anchor, both `suspendGapMs` and the plain Δmono; Δwall − Δmono > 5 s (`SUSPEND_GAP_THRESHOLD_MS`) **or** Δmono > 3 × the loop period (a monotonic clock that kept counting through a sleep, or a stalled event loop) both mean "suspended": abandon in-flight network calls and fetch before any apply or publish decision (01-engine §3.5 step 0a).
- The snapshot quiet period compares this PC's wall clock with this PC's file mtime only: a path whose mtime is < **300 ms** old is not read; a path whose mtime is in the future needs its stat unchanged for ≥ 300 ms Δmono.
- `meta.json` carries no wall-clock or monotonic timestamp (ENG-05); every timer restarts at daemon start.

**Snapshot and data:** deletion confirmation: missing in two reads ≥ **1 s** apart (Δmono); shrink guard: a > **50 %** shrink or a shrink to 0 bytes needs **2 stable snapshots** plus attribution (ruling R-25); staged read: `lstat`, one whole-file read, `lstat`, one re-read when `(size, mtime_ns)` changed, else UNSTABLE; an open or read error is UNREADABLE (01-engine §2). New-file cap **1,048,576** bytes; blobs ≥ **52,428,800** bytes (50 MiB) are SAVE_ONLY; binary = a NUL in the first **8,000** bytes; a new path ≤ **200** UTF-16 code units, each component ≤ **255** UTF-8 bytes; concat marker size `max(32, longest run + 1)` (glossary §20).

**Writer and apply:** ≤ 1 apply per **10 s** per worktree; free space ≥ **2 × bytes to write + 256 MB** (else `DEFERRED(disk_low)`); ≤ **3** re-merge retries per path, then the group defers `mismatch`; `.old` kept ≥ **2 s**; a gate query waits ≤ **250 ms** for an in-flight group; the writer is two-phase (rename target → `.old`, link `.new` → target) with the copy fallback only on a volume without hard links (glossary §26.1 W4, §26.2 item 7).

**Publish and governor (glossary §27.2, 02-protocol §3.1 and §3.8):** `B = protocol.json.budget` — 12 by default, seeded once at genesis from `host.pushBudgetPerMin` when it is an integer in 6–24, else 12 (ruling R-19); `G_s = ⌈60,000 / budget⌉ / 1,000` s and `S_s = ⌈18,000 / budget⌉ / 1,000` s (12 → 5 s, 1.5 s); local constants normal `(B_local, G_local, S_local) = (24, 2.5 s, 0.75 s)`, fallback `(6, 10 s, 3 s)`; `B_eff = min(B_local, budget)`, `G_base = max(G_local, G_s, 60 s / B_eff)`, `S_eff = max(S_local, S_s)`; throttle level k doubles `G_eff` up to **60 s**; per-peer cap `B_eff` attempts per 60 s Δmono; sliding team guard: no attempt while ≥ `B_eff` observed landings in the last 60 s Δmono; aging after 2 pre-emptions; fallback for **30 min** Δmono after a throttle signal (HTTP 429 or any 5xx on a push or fetch, a push taking > **10 s** Δmono or killed, a transient-rejection streak of **2**) or after folding a `HOST_THROTTLED` event; at most one `HOST_THROTTLED` event per **15 min** per peer; `NONRETRYABLE` at the **3rd** consecutive transient rejection of the same publish with the board unchanged; offline after > **30 s** without a successful fetch; network backoff **30 s doubling to 5 min**. Push only on change: no periodic, heartbeat or renewal push.

**Board:** caps **5,000** folded records, **5 MB** board tree, **1,000** ops and **64 KiB** per `ops.jsonl`, **16 KiB** per line; checkpoint at first-parent heights divisible by **256**; a joiner replays ≤ **255** commits; compaction drops terminal records with `terminal_height ≤ h − 512`; `K_evict` = **200** heights; `proto = 1`.

**State, incidents, convergence:** the state ref changes only by one CAS `update-ref` per transition; a CAS loss is a second writer (security log + exit, ENG-03); ≤ **3** displaced refs per branch; lost-state rebuild reads ≤ **500** first-parent commits; INV-CONVERGE: within **K = 20** loops after quiescence, then zero pushes (the SIM oracle runs **40** more loops).

**Status and lease:** `status.json` is written by temp file + rename on every iteration and transition, retried 3 × 50 ms on EPERM/EACCES then skipped; a heartbeat older than 3 loop periods means `down`; without a lease the loop stops syncing after **2 min** and drains and exits after **10 min** with `IDLE_NO_SESSION` (CCI-50, ENG-56).

**Tests and SIM:**
- Integration tests run real git against `file://` bare remotes (Plan 1 `createBareRemote`, `clonePeer`); git is never mocked; temp roots come from `makeTempRoot` (spaces and non-ASCII).
- SIM determinism (TST-03): the same seed tuple gives an identical op-log, trace and remote ref oids; every id comes from the peer's `seededRandom`; every commit carries `wallMsForDates = clock.wallMs()` (the engine passes it on every commit; the runner honours it only in test builds — Plan 7's L-DIST must keep that branch out of release bundles); generated edits set the file mtime to the peer's virtual wall clock (`utimes`) so the quiet-period rule compares one clock.
- Generated edits to "the same file, different lines" are separated by ≥ 2 unchanged lines: git's merge treats edits on adjacent lines as one conflict (measured on git 2.55 while planning: lines 1 and 2 of a 3-line file conflict; one unchanged line between them merges).
- CI per PR: 100 SIM seeds per leg (80 `edits` + 20 `faults`), distinct seed ranges for the two Node legs, so 200 seeds per OS per PR (glossary §20 CI row, 11-testing §3.11). The exit criterion run is `sim-exit.yml`: 1,000 `edits` seeds per OS.
- Message codes are the registry names verbatim (`^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]{2,47}$`); no rendered text reaches the board.
- Crashpoints: Plan 2 places exactly 14 names — the 13 of 01-engine ENG-59 plus `registry.cas` (HLD-01, two call sites); all are already in `test/crashpoints.json` (L-CRASHPOINTS).
- Yield points (Scheduler port; SIM interleaving): `publish.beforePush` (after the push intent is journaled, before `governedPush`), `apply.beforePhase2` (after P1 of a write group, before P2), `start.beforeGenesisPush` (after L0 and B0 are built, before their push). No other yield point is part of the contract.

**Holds (Plan 3):**
- **Plan 2 gate.** Plan 3 executes only on top of a merged Plan 2 (Task 14's fix round and Tasks 15–20 included, ruling R3-01). Every Plan 2 name this plan cites is used exactly as Plan 2's contract and ledger state it; a task that finds a cited name missing or different stops and reports.
- **One holding store** (HLD-01, HLD-12): every reason this PC's disk or outgoing tree differs from TIP plus this peer's ready, unheld publication groups is a record in `refs/cowork-local/<enc(b)>/holds`, except path-class masks (`QUARANTINED`, `SAVE_ONLY`, `UNMANAGED`), HLD-32 transient exclusions younger than 60 s, and an unreadable or unstable never-staged new file whose `h0` and `h1` are both ABSENT (nothing to hold; listed in `not_converged`, R3-56). No other ref, file, board record type or the `.old` ring holds bytes (lint L-HOLD-STORES). The board's `held/` tree is a mirror, never a store of record.
- **Record before displacement:** a write that removes local bytes from the disk (PARK, markers park, S6, the stop park, the collision take, a K4 displacement) runs only after the registry commit holding those bytes has passed its CAS; a record is never half-parked (HLD-16); a crash between the two is settled at restart by observation (DISK_EQ), never by guessing.
- **Every Plan 3 disk write goes through `Writer.writeLocal`/`writeLocalGroup`** with an origin (ENG-37) and is recorded as attributed (`ObservedWrites.record`; the park family and `--discard` also set the private-index entry and commit a snap-only state transition, R3-36, R3-60), so no detector counts it as an unobserved delta, in this run or after a restart; none runs while `view.intent.kind === 'push'` or `Writer.strandedPaths()` is non-empty (the operator reports `POSTPONED` and retries at a later loop, ruling R3-23); while any FREEZE reason is set only calls with `humanEvidence = true` write (`writeLocal` returns `FROZEN` otherwise).
- **Hold arithmetic** (HLD-13, 05 §3.4 preamble, DAT-40): every operator is Plan 2's `mergeFile` (`git merge-file -p --object-id` with MERGE_FLAGS through `runGit`); the base of REBASE and MASK is ENG's `A[p]`, never `tree(LA_C)[p]`; `--diff3` with the labels `cowork:disk`, `cowork:base`, `cowork:held` appears only in RESTORE; `--ours`, `--union` and `--theirs` never appear; no `merge-file` runs on a binary or filtered path (whole-path rules instead, ruling R3-18); every disk-versus-blob test is `DISK_EQ` (ENG-11).
- **`-U0` hunks** are computed and applied only in `src/holds/revert.ts`: `revertDetector` reads them, `taintOnlyH1` is the single applier and its output is only ever a record's `h1` (HLD-18, L-HOLD-STORES).
- **Objects (W6):** record content enters only through the staged-copy read with route (b) `-w`, or route (c) for `merge-file` outputs (stored form, ruling R3-19); MASK and REBASE run only in iterations that materialize or publish; a detection tick runs neither and writes no object; the revert detector runs only on a newly materialized `S_disk`, the mass-change breaker on every tick's dirty set (paths only) (ENG-14).
- **Claim-free** (HLD-06): no module that implements the revert detector, MASK, REBASE, PARK, RESTORE or R1 reads claim state; the only claim-dependent input is `PublishGatePort`.
- **Clocks:** a record's age comes only from its `age_anchor` (`max(Δwall since anchor_wall_ms, mono_accum_ms + Δmono of this run)`, INV-CLOCK); the 60 s transient clocks, the 30 s MASS window, the observed-write set and the edit-checkpoint quiet periods live in memory and restart at daemon start; Plan 3 adds no field to `meta.json` (ENG-05, R-14).
- **Board:** a `hold` op is ≤ **16 KiB** per line; `held/<hold_id>/<stage>/<repo path>` entries exist only for LIVE paths of non-`local_only` records, never for an ABSENT side; a `local_only` record's op is a content-free stub (its paths, no oid, no `held/` subtree); no new `held/` blob other than one already in `tree(TIP)` contains a restore-marker row (DAT-46); `hold` ops go board-only under FREEZE and the regionSpec VERSION pause and wait (queued, never dropped) under every other pause (PRO-45, HLD-10).
- **Codes:** every new message code Plan 3 introduces is a constant in `src/holds/codes.ts` (`HOLD_CODES` also declares four codes reserved for Plans 5–7, R3-65); Plan 3 also raises Plan 2's `FROZEN` and `UNFROZEN` unchanged; Plan 3 writes codes to `status.json` (`message_code`, `message_params`) and the daemon log only (R-27 carried); `HOLD_NOT_DURABLE` (removed in rev 2) never appears.
- **Names:** the 03-data incoming form check is `formCheckIncoming`, the 05-holds revert detector is `revertDetector`; neither is called "D2" alone in code, test titles or task titles.
- **Crashpoints and yield points:** Plan 3 places no new crashpoint name and no new yield point; its registry commits pass `registry.cas` and its disk writes pass the writer's existing anchored steps.
- **Shared helpers, never copied** (Plan 2 Task 14 ratification; the Task 17 carry "Never copy them"): `src/engine` code uses `src/engine/treeDiff.ts` (`diffPaths`, `sameTreeEntry`, `isRegularEntry`), `src/engine/treeEdit.ts` (`editTreeIndexOnly`, `editsBetween`, `editIndexOnly`, `INDEX_ONLY`) and `core.ts`'s `hooksDir` for every tree diff, index-only tree edit and private-index edit (`Snapshotter.setEntry` is `editIndexOnly` on the private index with `INDEX_ONLY`); `src/holds` (which cannot import `src/engine`) uses `src/git/scratchIndex.ts`'s `resetScratchIndex` before every `read-tree` into a scratch index (P2-SCRATCHLOCK) and takes tree diffs as caller-supplied parameters; no module keeps a private copy of any of them.

**Tests and SIM (Plan 3):**
- **CI per PR** (replaces Plan 2's "CI per PR" line): 100 SIM seeds per leg — 60 `edits` + 20 `faults` + 20 `holds` — with Plan 2's per-Node seed offsets, so 200 seeds per OS per PR; every SIM run asserts Plan 2's six exit oracles plus INV-NOLOSS, INV-MASK, INV-GROUP and the 05 oracle rows of Task 14. The exit criterion run is `sim-exit.yml`: 1,000 `holds` seeds per OS (and its existing 1,000 `edits` seeds).
- **SIM test time (R3-55):** a multi-seed SIM integration test runs its first seed under `npm test` and all its seeds under `npm run test:sim` (which `sim-exit.yml` runs per OS); every test file has a 10-minute `node:test` timeout; exit shards are sized from the per-OS per-seed time Task 16 measures on the PR legs (R3-58).
- Oracles recompute from git objects, board trees and working files with test-side code (their own `merge-file` calls, scratch indexes, file reads); an oracle never calls `maskEntries`, `writeGroups`, `HoldRegistry`, `isDurable` or any other engine function whose mutant it must catch.
- Every generated edit of the `holds` profile carries one fresh marker `CWM_<run>_<n>_<peer>` (own line or line suffix, placed safely in BOM, CRLF and cp949 files) (TST-08).
- Determinism (TST-03) covers records: ULIDs from the peer's seeded Random, `anchor_wall_ms` from the virtual clock, registry and board commits dated by `wallMsForDates`; Plan 3's trace events carry no duration and no wall time.

**Lines Plan 5 replaces (each still binds everywhere else):**
- "`dist/` stays the glossary §6 list; … the other five entry points stay stubs" → `dist/` stays the glossary §6 list (plus Plan 8a's `viz.js`, C14); Plan 5 turns `dist/hook.js`, `dist/core.js` and `dist/mcp.js` into the loader, the core and the MCP server and adds the `done` and `resolve` dispatch lines to `dist/cli.js` (R5-13); `dist/worker.js` stays Plan 4's. Every entry keeps `--version` and the no-argument no-op of Plan 1's `build.test.ts`.
- "No `child_process` outside `src/git/runner.ts` in `src/` (L-PORTS)" → also allowed in exactly two Plan 5 files: `src/claude/client/spawn.ts` (`spawnDaemon()`, CCI-51) and `src/sec/winAcl.ts` (the fixed-argv `whoami`/`icacls`/`Get-Acl` runner, SEC-20); no spawn call anywhere in the MCP tier (06 §3.2 lints, R5-03).
- "`process.hrtime` is read only in `src/ports/clock.ts`", L-FS-PORT and "Every read and write of user and cowork files goes through the `Fs` port" → the loader `src/entry/hook.ts` (bundled alone as `dist/hook.js`, ≤ 150 lines) may use only `node:*` built-ins: `node:fs` for the `.git` walk, the `live`/`paused` markers, the `seen-*` file and the `evidence.jsonl` line, `process.hrtime.bigint()` for its deadline, `node:crypto` `createHash`/`createHmac`; nothing else is exempt (lint L-LOADER, R5-03).
- Plan 3's "Plan 2 gate" → the base gate of R5-01 (merged Plans 2 and 3, 8a Tasks 2, 3 and 11; Plan 4 merged before Task 18).
- Plan 2's "Crashpoints: Plan 2 places exactly 14 names" and Plan 3's "no new crashpoint" → X-116: `test/crashpoints.json` is the single catalogue, each plan appends its names in its own commit, and the header assertion reads "equals the catalogue" (`lintCrashpoints`; F-P2-4 amends Plan 2's line); Plan 5 appends exactly one name, `deny.prerecord` (06 §3.6.6 step 3, 11 §3.6); no new yield point.
- Plan 3's "CI per PR" → Plan 5's line in "Tests and SIM (Plan 5)" below.

**Claude Code integration (Plan 5):**
- **Base gate (R5-01).** Plan 5 runs on top of merged Plans 2 and 3 (their exit evidence in their ledgers) and Plan 8a Tasks 2, 3 and 11; Task 1 re-baselines every Plan 1/2/3/8a name this contract cites (and F04-a's when landed) and a divergence stops the task for a controller ruling. Plan 4 is coded against through `RegionsPort` (bound to `noRegions` until Plan 4 merges); Task 16 starts only after F04-a has merged (R5-61); Task 18 starts only after Plan 4 has merged.
- **Hooks are advisory (CCI-01, S3).** No hook, MCP handler or CLI path pushes or publishes; the publish gate B1 and the verifying writer are the only safety boundaries; every claim-safety test asserts on the remote live code ref with `git cat-file`. A hook failure may cause a pause, a hold or a lost note, never a publish.
- **Fail closed.** For a gating event (edit-class PreToolUse, PreToolUse of `Bash|PowerShell|Monitor`) every failure the loader controls — a thrown exception, a rejected promise, malformed stdin, a `core.js` load error, an unreachable or slow daemon, a missed deadline — ends with exit 2 and the fixed G4 or load-failure text; every other event exits 0 with no output. Exit 1 is never used (INV-CCI-FAILCLOSED). `Agent|EnterWorktree` are decided locally and never take G4.
- **Never a permission decision (§1.3, CCI-11).** No output of any hook contains `permissionDecision` (any value); no `mcp_tool`, `http`, `prompt` or `agent` hook, no `WorktreeCreate` hook, no channel, no plugin monitor; `bashEditDiff` is never an enforcement input; only PostToolUse (K2) and Stop may use `decision: "block"` (L-HOOKS-JSON, L-PERMISSION, M26).
- **Gate from memory (CCI-12).** A gate request is answered from memory: no git process, no network call, no parse on the reply path; the only file read is the one short read of the target (pre-image, prediction input) and the only wait is the verifying writer's in-flight group on the same path (≤ 250 ms). Budgets over ≥ 200 calls: daemon ≤ 15 ms p95, end-to-end ≤ 100 ms p95 on Windows, event loop never blocked > 20 ms. Hook replies never wait for an edit check, an out-of-cycle iteration (`Engine.signal` is never awaited past queueing, R5-39), a confirmation (later results ride the next hook) or a fetch (except SUSPECT's ≤ 5 s under `extend`).
- **Record first (INV-CCI-RECORD-FIRST).** A K1 deny writes and fsyncs `<gitdir>/cowork/tmp/deny-<id>-<seq>.bin` (0600; `denyRecordPath`; one exclusive file per deny, the spec's `deny-<id>.bin` renamed by SE5-19) before the G1 reply (`crashpoint('deny.prerecord')`); a K2 block commits its PIN before the block reply; no record ever carries raw `tool_input`, `old_string`/`new_string`, prompt text, transcript paths or OS user names; every surviving `tmp/deny-*.bin` is converted at daemon start before anything else.
- **Hook reads (CCI-55, glossary §26.1).** Every read of a user's working file by Plan 5 code is one short read (open with full sharing, read the whole file in one call, close; `lstat` before and after, one re-read) on the `Fs` port; git never opens the user's file; bytes are hashed from bytes in hand by route (b) without `-w`; objects are written on a hook path only for hold records (deny shadows, K2 PINs) (M40, M41, O-STAGED).
- **Token custody (CCI-49, SEC-24, INV-TOKEN).** Only `src/claude/client/mcp/doorbell.ts` (in `dist/mcp.js`) reads `CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_TOKEN`, once, from its own environment into memory; it is written only in the `{"type":"auth"}` line on its own session's inbox socket; never over IPC, to disk, to a log or into hook output; hooks hash only the socket name (`seat_key`) and report only whether it is set; the daemon's environment is DAEMON_ENV (09 §3.4) and never contains a `CLAUDE_*` value; the daemon has no code path that opens an inbox socket (L-TOKEN, M27).
- **Privacy of frames (CCI-09, SEC-26).** No IPC frame carries the messaging token, prompt text, `command_args`, OS user names or transcript content; UserPromptSubmit sends only `nonces[]`; evidence and read-only grants carry arguments only as `args_sha256`; `tool_input` lives only in daemon memory and in a deny tmp file; read sets, read ranges and noticed sets never leave the daemon (no board op, ref, `status.json` field, event or doorbell); logs carry codes, typed params and ids only.
- **Text (CCI-45, glossary §19).** Plan 5 selects codes, carriers and typed params; Claude-facing text is produced only by `renderClaude` (R5-11), with every board-sourced string through Plan 8a's `sanitize(…, 'deny')` in G1 and K2 reasons and `sanitize(…, 'claude')` elsewhere; no free-form Claude-facing text exists outside the three loader texts; teammate free text never appears in a G1 stderr reason or a block reason; every carrier respects its cap (06 §3.14.1) and stays under the host's 2,000-char preview.
- **Human-only (SEC-25, INV-HUMANONLY).** The daemon executes an acting verb only with human evidence (a `tty` caller without a claude ancestor, or an unused UserPromptExpansion evidence record ≤ 60 s old, single use); no cowork process derives evidence from, or waits for, a UserPromptSubmit; no MCP tool moves a branch, releases another seat's claim, discards a hold, changes config, accepts a review item, acknowledges a secret-scan hit or writes a quarantined path (SEC-27).
- **Edit checkpoints are silent (CCI-56, D1).** No edit-checkpoint outcome produces a code, a carrier line, a board op, an event, a hold record or a log line naming a path or line; `last_check` lives in memory only (Plan 3's `ChangeSets.lastCheck`, X-05) and reaches a screen only through an all-paths `flow` reply with `detail: 'flow'` or a `wrap` reply, whoever asked (R5-14 as ruled by X-01).
- **Clocks (INV-CLOCK).** Every lease, TXN, budget, pairing, heartbeat, pid-check, doorbell-post, pending-wake, return-gap and throttle timer is a Δ on the daemon's monotonic clock (the 100 ms post observation on the MCP server's); loader deadlines count from loader process start; F04's return gap is this PC's `max(Δwall, Δmono)` through `ownStaleness`; no wall-clock value from another machine is ever used.
- **Codes.** Every code Plan 5 selects is a constant in `src/claude/wire/codes.ts`: `CCI_CODES` (06 §3.14.4, §1.2, 09 SEC_ codes and `NEW_CODES`) and `ROUTED_CODES` (the other chapters' registry codes Plan 5 selects or delivers — 05's hold codes, the status transition codes, 07's `STANDING_RULES`, 10's `RF_UNKNOWN_VERB`; R5-60); a status `message_code` passed through by a transition line is Plan 2/3's registry code and is not re-listed; the codes Plan 5 introduces (R5-34, R5-41, R5-42, R5-60) are flagged for registry entries (SE5-08); an overflow line is a template-only item (`T-MORE`, `T-STOP.more`, `T-AHEAD.more`), never a message code; `CS_NUDGE`, `SEC_QUARANTINE_HUMAN_ONLY` and `SEC_NOTE_REFUSED_SECRET` never appear.
- **Ports for later plans.** Every Plan 4/6/7 behaviour Plan 5 calls goes through a port declared in §Interfaces with a Plan 5 binding whose own unit test pins its stub behaviour; no Plan 5 module imports `src/regions/**` or `src/claims/**` except `src/claude/daemon/regions.ts`, and none imports a Plan 6 or Plan 7 module at all (X-110: Plans 6 and 7 come later in the order; they implement Plan 5's port types in their own files — Plan 6's `bindClaude`, Plan 7's note-scan/flow/wrap bindings — and `src/daemon/run.ts` spreads them into `ClaudeBindings`, X-111).

**Tests and SIM (Plan 5):**
- **CI per PR** (replaces Plan 3's line; X-113's one rule for every plan, asserted by `test/unit/ci-config.test.ts`): 100 SIM seeds per leg — every merged non-`edits` profile gets its final count (`faults` 15, `holds` 15, `claims` 15, `seats` 15, `coord` 15, `lifecycle` 10) and `edits` takes the remainder, in any merge order; Plan 5 adds the `seats` 15 — with Plan 2's per-Node seed offsets, so 200 seeds per OS per PR; every `seats` run asserts Plan 2's six exit oracles, Plan 3's ten and Plan 5's oracle set through the one options object `exitOracles(ns?, {holds: {markers}, claude: true})` (X-112: Plan 3 Task 16 declares `ExitOracleOptions`, Plan 5 appends `claude`; R5-15, Task 18). One FakeHost real-process leg per OS per PR runs the scenario list of Task 17; the latency gates (06 test 8, TC-CCI-03, TC-CCI-12) run on windows-latest only. The exit criterion run adds 1,000 `seats` seeds per OS to `sim-exit.yml` (its existing `edits` and `holds` runs unchanged).
- **SIM shape (R5-16).** In the seeded SIM a fake seat drives the same core and MCP-handler modules in process (hook JSON in, frames over the real `Ipc` port, the real daemon); seat ids, doorbell nonces and IPC handshake nonces come from the peer's `seededRandom`; loader deadlines and wall timing are never SIM decision inputs. The real-process FakeHost suite (`dist/hook.js`, `dist/mcp.js`, the Inbox stub) is not seeded and asserts the same four exit oracles over its own trace.
- Trace events Plan 5 adds carry no duration and no wall time (TST-03); lease and seat ages are computed by oracles from the trace recorder's test-side `monoAt(index)` table (Plan 3 Task 13).
- Every test that asserts a hook text asserts the code and typed params; text equality is asserted only for the three loader texts and the 07 rows Plan 5 seeds (R5-11).
- **Host-dependent work (TST-27, roadmap):** a PR implementing a host-dependent area merges only when every SPIKE-0 item that area depends on is PASS or FAIL-and-routed (status check `SPIKE0_INCOMPLETE`); real `claude -p` legs (TST-19) are Plan 7's nightly; nothing Plan 5 builds is released before the three human spikes of glossary §26.3 and Plan 8b (D4).

## Review Focus

The five conditions most likely to break in real use that no single task's tests would otherwise exercise (each has a test in the owning task):

1. **A broken install or a dead daemon on real Windows.** `core.js` deleted or truncated, the daemon binary removed, the daemon hung (NtSuspendProcess), a stale `status.json` heartbeat, a session whose `node` is not on PATH. Expected: every edit-class and every `Bash|PowerShell|Monitor` PreToolUse whose loader runs exits 2 within 1.5 s with the glossary §11 G4 text or 07's `T-HOOK_LOAD_FAIL` text, never exit 1; when `node` is not on PATH no loader runs, so the host fails open (ENOENT) and the write happens, attributed human-class (Task 17 asserts this cell); every non-gating hook exits 0 with no bytes; `Agent` without isolation still passes; the first non-gating hook of each seat after the daemon became unreachable carries `DAEMON_DOWN` exactly once; and a human-typed `/cowork pause` creates `<gitdir>/cowork/paused` through the loader alone, after which edits pass uncoordinated and the next SessionStart carries the PAUSED line. Pinned in Task 4 (`loader.faults` on the bundled `dist/hook.js`) and Task 17 (the FakeHost fault matrix on windows-latest, real processes).
2. **A human saving over a file Claude is editing.** An editor auto-save between PreToolUse and PostToolUse, an atomic-replace save, a 0-byte truncate-then-write save, and a 150 s permission prompt during which a teammate's change applies. Expected: an unstable pre-image is taken from the second read and the window marked approximate; an empty or > 50 % shrunk post-image confirms no claim, raises no K2, takes no edit checkpoint and records `last_check{skip: shrink}` until a second stable read ≥ 300 ms later; the expired INFLIGHT lease taints that path (`observedEdit` false) so the revert detector holds the reverted teammate line in a PATCH while the teammate's line stays in the live tip; no git process ever opens the working file. Pinned in Task 8 (`hookRead.saves` on the 1.3 MB BOM+CRLF fixture, the 06 test 19 expiry case), Task 10 (an unstable target read gives an INFLIGHT lease with `approximate: true` and the second read's bytes) and Task 12 (`post.emptyOrShrunk`).
3. **Long but healthy turns versus a dropped Post.** A 15-min test run inside a Bash TXN, 5 min at a permission prompt, Esc during a slow Write, a `run_in_background` server that keeps writing. Expected: never DEGRADED and no lease beyond its cap (TXN closes at 120 s, later writes are human-class tagged with the seat, the incoming change to a fenced path lands within 125 s of the fence); one PostToolUse whose Pre never reached the daemon makes the seat DEGRADED once, defers only that seat's 30-min read and edit sets, leaves another seat's applies running and recovers silently after 3 matched pairs. Pinned in Task 9 (`health.degraded` with its negative variants) and Task 8 (`leases.caps`).
4. **Session churn on a held claim.** Claim `foo()`, then `/clear`, `/compact`, in-process `/resume`, `/fork`, kill the MCP server and reconnect with `/mcp`, then open a second Claude window on the same repo. Expected: every Edit of `foo()` by the first seat passes (no self-deny), its digest starts with `DIGEST_SUPERSEDES`, no lease of an earlier `session_id` survives its SessionEnd, the MCP death gives exactly one `HOOK_ONLY` notice and gating unchanged, the reconnect returns the wiring to `FULL` with the same `seat_id`, the second window is "session 2" and is denied on `foo()` like a teammate (REG-19). Pinned in Task 5 (`seats.continuity`), Task 17 (FakeHost `churn` scenario, real processes) and Task 18 (the self-deny and sibling-deny half with Plan 4's claims).
5. **Human verbs racing doorbells and Claude's own CLI calls.** A human types `/cowork save "x"` while a doorbell is pending for the seat; a human types `/cowork done` and the `!` line starts before the UserPromptExpansion evidence lands; Claude runs `node …/cli.js save` and `node …/cli.js status --flow` from Bash 10 s after the human's `/cowork status --flow`. Expected: the pending-wake expansion mints nothing and the CLI refuses `SEC_REFUSE_NOT_HUMAN` naming the command to retype; the late evidence is found within the 3 s wait and `done` runs once; Claude's `save` is denied exit 2 (`SHELL_COWORK_CLI`) and, if run anyway, refused for lack of evidence; the human's slash `status --flow` and Claude's Bash `status --flow` both show `last_check` (the daemon does not test who asked, D1 final, X-01) and nothing about it reaches a carrier, doorbell or mirror, while plain `status` and `show <path>` carry `last_check: null` with `last_withheld` (O10 entry, no `last_check`); a `/cowork since` whose `!` line starts before its expansion's read-only grant lands still moves the `last-run` mark when the grant lands within the same 3 s wait, and Claude's Bash `cli.js since` never does (FEAT-F04-18, X-02). Pinned in Task 15 (`acting.races`, T-P5-flow-last-acceptance), Task 16 (the `since_ran` grant cells), Task 17 (the FakeHost `verbs` variant with a delayed expansion hook) and Task 10 (the `SHELL_COWORK_CLI` deny).
